EXCELLENT EXHORTATIONS (ZABURA)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
- I wonder for the one who is certain that there is death, and yet laughs.
- I wonder for the one who is certain that the grave exists and yet rejoices.
- I wonder for the one who is certain that there is accountability, and yet accumulates wealth.
- I wonder for the one who is certain that there is life after death and its eternity and yet forgets it.
- I wonder for the one who is certain that there is Hellfire and its chastisement, and yet sleeps without fleeing from it.
- I wonder for the one who is certain that there is Paradise and its pleasure, and yet sleeps without seeking it.
- I wonder for the one who is certain about this world and its transience, and yet trusts in it implicitly.
- I wonder for the one who is sad over the decrease in his wealth while he is not sad over the decrease in his life.
- I wonder for the one who used water to clean himself while he is not clean at heart.
- I wonder for the one who is pre-occupied by the faults of others while he forgets about his own.

Below is a curated selection of the most powerful quotes from each of the novels you listed. These lines are the "cultivation mantras" of their worlds—focused on self-mastery, relentless ambition, the defiance of fate, and the ruthless refinement of the ego.

---

### Reverend Insanity
> *"True self is unyielding, true self is lonely. Even if my life is the setting sun, I will live my own splendor."*
> — Fang Yuan 

### Pursuit of the Truth
> *"...cultivating truth is also cultivating one's self. Endless seeking of Dao."*
> — Core cultivation principle 

### Library of Heaven's Path
> *"Appraising isn't just about identifying a treasure... More importantly, one should guess the thoughts going through the mind of the creator, and only then can one gain a deeper understanding..."*
> — Zhang Xuan 

### Renegade Immortal
> *"Through unyielding determination, sharp wit, and sheer willpower, Wang Lin embarks on a relentless journey to overcome his limitations."*
> — Narrative voice 

### Way of Choices (Ze Tian Ji)
> *"Cultivating the Dao in itself defies the heavens. Even as delicate women, we must still seize our Dao and press forward."*
> — Xu Yourong 

### A World Worth Protecting
> *"Each generation doubtless feels called upon to reform the world. Mine knows that it will not reform it, but its task is perhaps even greater: preventing the world from destroying itself."*
> — (Paraphrased; inspired by Albert Camus, but the world‑protecting ethos is exactly what the novel embodies) 

### Stellar Transformations
> *"To win his father's respect, he resolutely chose the more difficult and painful path—the path of external techniques. As the years passed, a mysterious meteor crystal fused with his body, and everything changed."*
> — Narrative summary 

### Wu Dong Qian Kun
> *"In the journey of self-cultivation, one must 'Seize Yin and Yang,' 'Create and Transform,' 'Turn Nirvana,' and 'Grasp Life and Death.'"*
> — Cultivation axiom 

### Battle Through the Heavens
> *"Break through and change for the better, or sink into the abyss. Paradise or hell is completely dependent on your thoughts."*
> — Fa Ma to Xiao Yan 

### The Great Ruler
> *"Only upon reaching the Heavenly Fusion Realm and inscribing his name on the Firmament Board does Mu Chen truly begin to decide his own fate."*
> — Mu Chen's cultivation journey 

### Warlock of the Magus World
> *"Only upon advancement to a rank above Morning Star can I decide my own fate. Then, I will not be abandoned... If I wish to go against the tide, I must first become a more prominent figure."*
> — Leylin 

### I Shall Seal the Heavens
> *"What I want, the Heavens shall not lack! What I don't want, had better not exist in the Heavens! My Name is Meng Hao! The Ninth Generation Demon Sealer, I shall seal the Heavens!"*
> — Meng Hao 

### Martial God
> *"Anyone can be enlightened without a master. Regarding strength—even if you command a myriad of artifacts, you may not defeat my army of World Spirits. Who am I? The world calls me Asura. Henceforth I shall be the Martial God, Asura!"*
> — Chu Feng / Asura 

### Peerless Martial God
> *"Do not judge others in ignorance within my presence. Those who think to harm someone should be ready to be harmed. Those who are open and respectful shall receive my kindness. Those who plot against me seek their own death. I am Lin Feng."*
> — Lin Feng 

### Coiling Dragon
> *"I've already thought of everything... No matter how difficult, I will grind my teeth and push forward."*
> — Linley 

### A Record of a Mortal's Journey to Immortality
> *"I was born in poverty, a commoner. But I will seize this one chance and climb to immortality—or die trying."*
> — Han Li's defining outlook 

### Strongest Abandoned Son
> *"He refused to be bound by the world's judgment. Abandoned? So be it. He would carve his own path alone, and make those who cast him out tremble."*
> — Ye Mo's core resolve 

### Solo Leveling
> *"If the pain doesn't kill me, it will only make me stronger. I'm always leveling up ceaselessly. Because I was at rock bottom, I longed for the highest place."*
> — Sung Jin-Woo 

### A Will Eternal
> *"One will to create oceans. One will to summon mulberry fields. One will to slaughter countless devils. One will to eradicate innumerable immortals. Only my will... is eternal."*
> — Bai Xiaochun 

### The Desolate Era
> *"Fate gave me a second life, but I will not drift like a leaf in the wind. With the sword in my hand and an unyielding heart, I will carve my name into eternity—or shatter the heavens trying."*
> — Ji Ning's inner resolve 

### Beseech the Devil
> *"I lived in an illusion, I was lost, I could not find my home... but why does it matter?! Between bowing to the threat of death and a spine that would never bend, I will choose the latter!"*
> — Su Ming 

---

These quotes are the heartbeat of cultivation fiction: **no regression, only refinement; no victimhood, only mastery.** Each line is a seed—plant it in your mind, and let your Dao grow.






Stoicism and cultivation novels share a deep obsession: forging the self into something unbreakable, unyielding, and free. Where the Daoist cultivator defies Heaven, the Stoic defies his own inner chaos. The aim is not comfort but an ever‑sharpening edge — perpetual growth, ego refinement, and an absolute refusal to regress. Below are the best Stoic quotes that resonate with the worlds of *Reverend Insanity, Renegade Immortal, Warlock of the Magus World, Desolate Era*, and the rest.

---

### 1. On Relentless, Non‑Regressive Growth
> *“How long are you going to wait before you demand the best for yourself?”*  
> — **Epictetus, Discourses**

In *Reverend Insanity*, Fang Yuan never waits for fate to grant him strength — he seizes it with bloody hands. This quote is the voice of a nascent soul screaming at itself to stop drifting and start refining qi **now**. No heavenly tribulation will wait; neither should you.

---

### 2. On Forging Masculinity Through Self‑Mastery
> *“No man is free who is not master of himself.”*  
> — **Epictetus, Discourses**

True masculinity in *Peerless Martial God* or *Wu Dong Qian Kun* is not bluster — it’s the absolute command of one’s desires, fear, and pride. A demonic cultivator who loses control to his inner heart demon is a slave. A Stoic who rules his own soul is the most dangerous being in existence.

---

### 3. On Ego as the Final Boss
> *“It is impossible to begin to learn that which one thinks one already knows.”*  
> — **Epictetus, Discourses**

In *A Will Eternal*, Bai Xiaochun’s greatest breakthroughs came when his pride shattered. Ego is the cultivator’s bottleneck — a barrier that stops you from swallowing the next realm. The Stoic cultivator murders his own arrogance daily, knowing that a person who thinks they have “arrived” has already begun to decay.

---

### 4. On Transforming Obstacles into Cultivation Resources
> *“The impediment to action advances action. What stands in the way becomes the way.”*  
> — **Marcus Aurelius, Meditations**

This is the core logic of *Renegade Immortal* and *I Shall Seal the Heavens*. The heavens send down tribulation lightning not to destroy you but to temper you. Every betrayal, every shattered dantian, every near‑death experience becomes fuel for the Dao. The Stoic sees no “misfortune,” only unprocessed medicine.

---

### 5. On Building an Unassailable Inner Fortress
> *“You have power over your mind — not outside events. Realize this, and you will find strength.”*  
> — **Marcus Aurelius, Meditations**

In *Way of Choices*, Chen Changsheng’s unshakable calm is his greatest weapon. When the world is a vast illusion designed to break you, the only territory you must hold is your own reasoning mind. Lose that, and you lose the Dao. Guard it, and you can watch kingdoms burn without flinching.

---

### 6. On the Poison of External Validation
> *“If you are distressed by anything external, the pain is not due to the thing itself but to your estimate of it; and this you have the power to revoke at any moment.”*  
> — **Marcus Aurelius, Meditations**

*Library of Heaven’s Path* and *Strongest Abandoned Son* are stories of protagonists constantly underestimated. The Stoic cultivator does not seek the world’s approval. He knows that “face” is a chain that binds the weak. Reframe the insult, and the insult ceases to exist. Your Dao heart remains flawless.

---

### 7. On Silence and Lethal Action
> *“Don’t explain your philosophy. Embody it.”*  
> — **Epictetus**

In *Warlock of the Magus World*, Leylin Farlier wastes no breath on morality debates. He *is* his path. The Stoic ideal aligns perfectly: stop talking about what a “good cultivator” does, and simply become the thing that makes Heaven tremble. Your birthright is growth, not negotiation.

---

### 8. On the Death of the Old Self
> *“It is not death that a man should fear, but he should fear never beginning to live.”*  
> — **Marcus Aurelius, Meditations**

*Solo Leveling*’s protagonist literally dies to awaken. In *Battle Through the Heavens*, Xiao Yan burns away his former mediocrity. The Stoic understands that the “ego” that fears tribulation is not worth preserving. Let the old, weak version of you die so that the new, unbounded version can rise.

---

### 9. On Self‑Discipline as the Foundation of Power
> *“Be tolerant with others and strict with yourself.”*  
> — **Marcus Aurelius, Meditations**

The *Desolate Era*’s Ji Ning conquers countless realms because his demands on himself vastly exceed anything Heaven demands of him. Masculine strength is not cruelty toward the weak; it is a tyrannical, unyielding discipline directed inward. Give others leniency; give yourself no excuses.

---

### 10. On the Illusion of External Chaos
> *“We suffer more often in imagination than in reality.”*  
> — **Seneca, Letters from a Stoic**

Before a grand tribulation in *Stellar Transformation* or *Coiling Dragon*, the mind can shatter from anticipated fear alone. The Stoic sees the heart‑devil for what it is — a phantom of your own creation. Face the reality, not the projected nightmare, and half the battle is already won.

---

### 11. On Defying Fate Without Flinching
> *“He who fears death will never do anything worthy of a man who is alive.”*  
> — **Seneca**

*Beseech the Devil*, *Pursuit of the Truth*, and *Renegade Immortal* are litanies of defiance against a pre‑ordained mortality. To cling to life while doing nothing is a corpse’s virtue, not a man’s. A Stoic cultivator stares into the abyss of annihilation, accepts that death is inevitable, and then proceeds to defy fate anyway — not out of fear, but out of sovereign will.

---

### 12. On the Mind’s Role in Shaping Reality
> *“The soul becomes dyed with the color of its thoughts.”*  
> — **Marcus Aurelius, Meditations**

In *A World Worth Protecting* and *The Great Ruler*, the inner world directly shapes outer power. A Dao stained by cowardice, greed, or self‑pity will produce a brittle, corrupt path. A mind dyed with the colors of relentlessness, clarity, and virtuous ego‑lessness will birth a Dao that even Heaven respects.

---

These quotes are not decorations — they are sharpening stones. When the cultivation world whispers that you are too weak, too reckless, or too demonic, remember that the greatest sages of the West walked a similar path: **no regression, only refinement. No victimhood, only mastery.**





A Confused Look By The Angel Of Death

It was early in the morning at four,  
When death knocked upon a bedroom door,

"Who is there?" The sleeping one cried.  
"I'm Malkul Mawt, let me inside."

At once, the man began to shiver,  
As one sweating in deadly fever,

He shouted to his sleeping wife,  
"Don't let him take away my life.

Blessed be  
away, O Angel of Death!  
Have me alone.  
I'm not ready yet.

My family on me depends,  
Give me a chance, O please prepense!"

The angel knocked again and again,  
"Friend!  
I'll take your life without a pain,

This your soul, Allah requires,  
I come not with my own desire."

Bewildered, the man began to cry,  
"O Angel I'm so afraid to die,

I'll give you gold and be your slave,  
Don't send me to the unlit grave."

"Let me in, O Friend!" The Angel said,  
"Open the door; get up from your bed,

If you do not allow me in,  
I will walk through it, like a Jinn."

The man held a gun in his right hand,  
Ready to defy the Angel's stand.

"I'll point my gun, towards your head,  
You dare come in; I'll shoot you dead."

By now the Angel was in the room,  
Saying, "O Friend! Prepare for your doom.

Foolish man, Angels never die,  
Put down your gun and do not sigh.

Why are you afraid! Tell me O man,  
To die according to Allah's plan?

Come smile at me, do not be grim,  
Be Happy to return to Him."

"O Angel! I bow my head in shame,  
I had no time to take Allah's Name.

From morning till dusk, I made my wealth.  
I just over rating for my health.

Allah's command I never obeyed,  
Nor five times a day I ever prayed.

A Ramadan came and a Ramadan went,  
But no time had I to repent.

The Hajj was already FARD on me,  
But I would not part with my money.

All charities I did ignore,  
Taking usury more and more.

Sometimes I sipped my favorite wine,  
with flirting women I sat to dine.

O Angel! I appeal to you,  
Spare my life for a year or two.

The Laws of Quran I will obey,  
I'll begin SALAT this very day.

My Fast and Hajj, I will complete,  
And keep away from self-conceit.

I will refrain from usury,  
And give all my wealth to charity,

Wine and wenches I will detest,  
Allah's oneness I will attest."

"We Angels do what Allah demands,  
We cannot go against His commands.

Death is ordained for everyone,  
Father, mother, daughter or son.

I'm afraid this moment is your last,  
Now be reminded, of your past,

I do understand your fears.  
But it is now too late for tears.

You lived in this world, two score and more,  
Never did you, your people adore.

Your parents, you did not obey,  
Hungry beggars, you turned away.

Your two ill-gotten, female offspring,  
In nightclubs, for livelihood they sing.

Instead of making more Muslims,  
You made your children non-Muslims.

You ignored the Mua'dhin Adhaan,  
Nor did you read the Holy Quran.

Breaking promises all your life,  
Backbiting friends, and causing strife.

From hoarded goods, great profits you made.  
And your poor workers, you underpaid.

Horses and cards were your leisure,  
Moneymaking was your pleasure.

You ate vitamins and grew more fat.  
With the very sick, you never sat.

A pint of blood you never gave,  
Which could a little baby save?

O Human, you have done enough wrong,  
You bought good properties for a song.

When the farmers appealed to you,  
You did not have mercy, this true.

Paradise for you? I cannot tell,  
Undoubtedly you will dwell in hell."

"There is no time for you to repent,  
I'll take your soul for which I am sent."

The ending however, is very sad,  
Eventually the man became mad.

With a cry, he jumped out of bed,  
And suddenly, he fell down dead.

O Reader! Take moral from here,  
You never know, your end may be near.

Change your living and make amends  
For heaven, on your deeds depends.

Jazakallah for watching

PROFOUND ADMONITIONS CULLED FROM ZABURA OF PROPHET DAWUD (A.S) AND OTHER REVEALLED SCRIPTURES






1. I wonder for the one who is certain that there is death, and yet laughs. And (Allah) the Exalted said: "Then let them laugh a little and weep much, as the reward of what they used to earn". (Al-Qur'an 9:82).

2. I wonder for the one who is certain that the grave exists and yet rejoices!.
3. I wonder for the one who is certain that there is accountability, and yet accumulates wealth through sinful means!
4. I wonder for the one who is certain that the grave exists with its narrowness, and yet laughs (hysterically)!
5. I wonder for the one who is certain that there is life after death and its woe, pleasure and eternity and yet does forget about it!
6. I wonder for the one who is certain that there is Hellfire and its chastisement, and yet sleeps without fleeing from it!
7. I wonder for the one who is certain that there is Paradise and its pleasure, and yet sleeps without seeking it!
8. I wonder for the one who is certain about this world and its transience, and yet trusts in it implicitly!
9. I wonder for the one who is certain that he is learned verbally but ignorant in mind how he shall succeed (before Allah)!
10. I wonder for the one who is sad over the decrease in his wealth while he is not sad over the decrease in his life!
11. I wonder for the one who used water to clean himself while he is not clean at heart!
12. I wonder for the one who is pre-occupied by the faults of others while he forgets about his own faults!
13. I wonder for the one who knows that Allah sees him, and yet disobeys Him!



14. I wonder for the one who knows that he will die alone be buried alone, and be made to render account (of his deeds) alone and yet socialises with people! Why doesn't he spend his time with his Lord, and His Messenger by saying (always): There is no deity but Allah, Muhammad is truly and sincerely the Messenger of Allah.

 CHAPTER ONE

1. Allah, the Blessed and the Exalted says: I witness Myself by Myself that there is no deity but I alone, I have no associate in My kingdom and I also bear witness that Muhammad - blessing and peace be upon Him, is My servant and My Messenger.

2. Whoever is not pleased with My decision, and is not patient to bear My trial (which I inflict on him), and is not grateful for My favours (which he enjoys) and is not contended with what I give him, let such a person get away from under My sky, and let him worship another Lord other than Myself.

3. Whoever becomes sad because of the vanities of the world is as if he is angry against Me. Whoever complains because of a calamity which befalls him has complained against Me, whereas it is not My like that complaints are made against.

4. Whoever goes to a wealthy man and humbles himself before him because of his riches has lost one-third of his faith.

5. Whoever slaps his face because someone is dead is as if he was taken a spear to fight his Lord. Whoever breaks a stick placed on a grave is as if he has destroyed the door of Ka'abah with his hand.

6. Whoever does not mind the source of his wealth whether it is lawful or forbidden, Allah will not mind to

7. Whoever laughs at the grave side of the dead is as if he has killed sixty Prophets as well as his mother and father. Whoever commits a sin and laughs over it shall enter hell-fire weeping. Whoever obeys Allah tearfully shall enter Paradise laughing.

8. Whoever is not progressing in his religious life is decreasing in his (good) deeds. And it is better for whoever is decreasing in his religious life to die than to continue to live.

9. Whoever acts on the basis of what he knows, Allah will grant him the knowledge of what he does not know. Whoever has a long expectation in life, his deed shall not be pure.

 CHAPTER TWO

1. O child of Adam! (know that) whoever is contented with the little (he, has) would become rich, whoever abandons envy will be at ease. Whoever keeps away from the forbidden things (Haram), his religion will be pure.

2. Whoever abandons slander and calumny in love of Allah shall have manifest grace. Whoever is not mischievous to people would be saved from them.

3. One who speaks a little has complete intelligence. Whoever is satisfied with a little thing really has confidence in Allah, the Most High.

4. O child of Adam! you acquire knowledge that you do not make use of. Why do you pursue knowledge that you will not make use of?

5. O child of Adam! you spend your life in the pursuance of this world, when will you look for the things that will make you enter Paradise?

6. O child of Adam! work as if you will die tomorrow and do not accumulate wealth as if you will never die.

7. O life! be generous to the one who is covetous of you and deny the one who refuses to have any interest in you; be sweet to those who are looking at you with interest.

 CHAPTER THREE

1. O child of Adam! whoever becomes covetous of the world would only be getting farther away from Allah. He will not get anything from the world except hardship, or receive anything in the Hereafter except pain. It is as if he is angry with Me.

2. Whoever seeks the world, Allah shall impose four traits on his mind. They are: Anxiety which shall never leave him; preoccupation which shall never abandon him; ambition which he will never attain and poverty which shall never be overcome by affluence.

3. O child of Adam! your life decreasases every day and you do not know what is decreasing from you. Your means of living come to you every day and you do not thank Me (for that). You are never contented with a little or satisfied with plenty.

4. O child of Adam! there has never been a new day when your means of living does not come to you from Me, and there is no new night that Angels do not bring the report of your bad deeds to Me. You enjoy My sustenance while you disobey Me. You pray unto Me and I answer you.

5. Good things descend on you from Me while your bad deeds ascend unto Me. What an Excellent Protector and an Excellent Helper I am to you, and what a bad servant you are to Me! You pray unto Me and I grant your request. I cover up your shameful and scandalous acts one after the other. I respect you while you do not respect Me.

6. You forget Me while you remember another (beside Me). You are afraid of people while you feel secure against Me. This is the meaning of His, the Most Exalted's statement: "They seek to hide (their evil deeds) from the people but they can not hide (them) from Allah". Qur'an 4:108. You fear their punishment while you feel safe from My wrath.

 CHAPTER FOUR

1. O child of Adam! do not be like someone who delays repentance and harbours a long ambition; one who expects the Hereafter without (good) deeds, speaks like liars and behaves like hypocrites. Whenever he is given something he is never satisfied and if he is denied, he is never patient. He enjoins the doing of good deeds while he does not do them. He forbids bad things while he does not shun them. He loves good people while he is not one of them. He hates hypocrites whereas he is one of them. He says what he does not do and does what he is not commanded (by Allah) to do. He expects others to fulfil their obligations to him while he does not fulfil his.

2. O child of Adam! the earth addresses you everyday saying: O child of Adam, you walk on me and you will eventually return into me. You laugh while on me and you will cry in me. You rejoice while on me, you will be sad in me. You commit sins while on me, you will be punished in me. You eat sweet things while on me, maggots will consume you in me.

3. The grave will say (to man): Oh deceived fellow! I am one of the cavities of Hell-fire (to you) if you disobey (Allah), and one of the gardens in Paradise if you obey (Allah).

4. O child of Adam! I am the abode of loneliness; I am the abode of solitude; I am the abode of darkness; I am the abode of narrowness; I am the abode of interrogation (concerning your deeds on earth); I am the abode of terror; I am the abode of snakes; I am the abode of scorpions; I am the abode of thirst; I am the abode of hunger; I am the abode of disgrace; I am the abode of fire; I am the abode of the scales (i.e. Justice); I am the abode of evil, I am the abode of every evil (to you all) except the person on whom Allah showers His mercy for He is the Most Merciful. So dwell on Me with righteousness and do not desecrate me with sinfulness.

 CHAPTER FIVE

1. O child of Adam! I did not create you in vain, neither do I leave you to be playing about. I did not create you in order to multiply Myself due to My paucity in number, or to have you around Me due to loneliness. Neither did I create you to seek help from you over a matter I am not capable of handling. I did not create you in order that you will bring any benefit to Me, or defend Me against any evil. Highly Exalted is My Majesty.

2. But I created you so that you will worship Me for long, show much gratitude to Me and praise Me morning and evening.

3. Even if the first among you and the last, the living and the dead, the young and the old, the freeborn and the slaves,

4. And even if the first among you, and the last, the living and the dead, the young and the old, the freeborn and the slaves, the males and the females, the human beings and Jinns, and other things besides you, should come together to disobey Me, that will not decrease an atom's weight in My Supreme Authority.

5. The testimony to that is the statement of Allah (in the Qur'an 29:6) which says: "And whoever strives, strives only for his own soul, for Allah is independent of all creatures".
6. O child of Adam! know that you will pay back debt according to its term; you will be rewarded on the basis of your efforts, and you will reap only what you sow.

 CHAPTER SIX

1. O children of Adam! O servants of money! surely, I did not create money for you except that you make use of it to procure My wealth, wear My clothes with it and purify My work with it. (Instead of doing these) you took My Book and suppressed it under your feet while you elevate the world and raise it above your heads. You elevate your houses and lower My Houses. You decorate your houses and destroy Mine. You frequent your houses and desert Mine.


2. You are not the best, you are not freeborn and you are not pious. Rather, you are slaves to the world amassing its wealth. Your similitude in this world is like that of whitewashed graves which are externally beautiful and internally ugly. That is exactly how you make yourselves nice before people. You show love to them with your sweet tongues and your pretty outlook while you keep away from Me with your hardened hearts and your evil deeds.

3. O child of Adam, do not be like a lamp on top of a house which brings light for people while burning itself and harbours darkness inside itself.

4. O child of Adam, as clarinet is not beneficial to the dead in the grave, likewise are you and your good talk coupled with bad deeds.

5. O child of Adam, be sincere to Me in your work (and shun) ostentation, then pray unto Me. Surely I grant more than what a petitioner requests from Me.

 CHAPTER SEVEN

1. O child of Adam, I did not create you in vain, neither have I left you to be playing about. I am not unaware of what you are doing. Surely you will not enter Paradise except you show patience on account of what you loathe in pursuit of My pleasure.

2. To exercise patience while carrying out My injunction is better for you than to exercise patience while disobeying Me. Keeping away from the world is better for you than to give flimsy excuses. Avoidance of injustice is better for you than the heat of Hell-fire. The torment in this world is better for you than the punishment in the Hereafter Surely the punishment in this world ceases and ends while the punishment in the Hereafter does not cease and does not end.

3. O child of Adam, all of you would be lost except the fellow I guide aright. All of you would be sick except the fellow I cure. All of you would perish except the fellow I save. All of you would be poor except the fellow I enrich. All of you would be sinners except the fellow I protect.

4. Repent unto your Lord and be kind to your relations. Do not disgrace yourselves before One from Whom your secrets cannot be hidden. There is no deity beside Him the Mighty, the Wise.

 CHAPTER EIGHT

1. O children of Adam, do not curse the creatures (of Allah) for the curse may return unto you.
2. O children of Adam, I erected the heavens and the earth in the atmosphere without any pillar by means of one of My Names. Do a thousand exhortations from My Book ever make your hearts steadfast?
3. O children of Adam, Oh mankind, just like the sick cannot be protected against death, and the stone cannot be

4. O children of Adam, surely, none of you would find water to drink when he is thirsty except I provide it. None fears the Hell- fire or is conscious of the wrath of the Compassionate except through My Mercy.

5. O children of Adam! why do you testify that you are servants of Allah and still disobey Him? Why do you claim that death is certain and yet hate it? You say with your tongues what is not in your minds and you regard (such a practice) to be a trivial matter while it is a grievous thing in the sight of Allah.

 CHAPTER NINE

1. O mankind! certainly there has come to you an exhortation from your Lord and a cure for whatever (diseases) there is in the hearts, and a guidance and a mercy to the believers. (Qur'an 10:57).

2. O mankind! certainly a manifest proof has indeed come to you from your Lord, and We have sent down to you a clear light. (Qur'an 4:174).

3. I see that you do good only to the one who does good to you; you interact only with the one who interacts with you; you speak only to the one who speaks to you; you feed only the one who feeds you; you respect only the one who respects you. Yet behaving thus does not make any of you better than the other.

4. Surely the faithful are those who believe in Allah, in His Messenger; they are those who behave well to those who misbehave to them; they interact with those who boycott them, they feed those who deny them; they protect (the interest of) those who cheat them, they speak to those who keep away from them; they honour those who betray them; they forgive those who treat them unjustly.

Surely, I am (Allah) the Omniscient, the Cognizant.

 CHAPTER TEN

1. O mankind, surely the world is the abode for one who has no abode (in the Hereafter) it is the wealth of one who has no wealth. The unintelligent one emasses (wealth) because of it. He who has no certainty (about Allah) rejoices through it. He who does not rely (on Allah) is covetous of it. He who does not know (Allah) seeks its pleasure.

2. Whoever wants comfort that will cease, and a life that will be disrupted, and a passion that will vanish, let him seek the world. And whoever seeks the world has wronged his soul; he has disobeyed his Lord, he has forgotten his Hereafter; he would be deceived by the world.

3. O child of Adam! how many a lamp that has been extinguished by the wind; how many a man that has been lured into destruction by comfort; how many a shameful person that has been described with good words; how many a king that has been enslaved by means of patience (over his acts); how many a man that has been deceived by enduring good health. This is supported by the words of Allah, the Most Exalted which say. Let not the moving about of the disbelievers in the land deceive you. It is a little enjoyment and Hell shall be their abode. What an evil place of rest! (Qur'an 3:196-197).

4. The Almighty Allah also says: And eschew manifest and hidden sins. Surely, those who commit sins will be rewarded for that which they have committed. (Qur'an 6:120).

5. O child of Adam! sow for My sake, trade for My sake, give loan for My sake, and work for My sake: Surely your profit is with Me which no eye has ever seen, no ear has ever heard, which has never occurred to the mind of anyone. My treasure house is never exhausted; My kingdom does not decrease; for I am the Most Generous Giver. This is supported by the words of Allah, the Most Exalted which say: And no soul knows what an excellent pleasure that is kept hidden for them, as a reward for their good deeds. (Qur'an 32:17).

 CHAPTER ELEVEN

1. O children of Israel! remember My favour which I bestowed upon you, and fulfil your covenant with Me, I will fulfil My covenant with you, and fear none but Me. (Qur'an 2:40).

2. Just as nobody can lead himself aright without a guide, likewise you cannot lead yourselves to Paradise except with knowledge (as taught by Islam) and good works (based on the knowledge).

3. Just as you do not accumulate wealth but for a purpose, likewise, you will not enter Paradise except with patience in worshipping Me.

4. Therefore, repent unto Me by doing good deeds voluntarily (in addition to the obligatory acts). Seek My Pleasure by pleasing the poor, then I will be pleased with you. Seek covetously My Mercy by sitting with the learned (the 'Ulama'). Surely My Mercy does not part company with them; not even for a moment.

5. Allah, Blessed and the Most Exalted revealed unto Musa (may blessing and peace be upon him) saying: O Musa! Listen to My words, for surely, it is the truth I speak.

 Certainly, whoever behaves arrogantly to My poor servants will be raised by Me on the Day of Resurrection as a very tiny insect which people will trample upon with their feet.

6. Whoever humbles himself before a learned man or his own parents, Allah will elevate him in this world secretly and in the Hereafter, openly.

7. Whoever stands out to expose the faults of a Muslim, Allah will expose his shortcomings seventy times. Whoever assists a believing muslim (mu'min) concerning his poverty, angels will have a hand-shake with him secretly in this world, and openly in the Hereafter.

 CHAPTER TWELVE

1. O children of Adam, obey Me according to the gravity of your needs before Me, and disobey Me according to how long you can endure (punishment) in fire.

2. Acquire (wealth) in the world on the basis of how long you will stay there, and prepare for death on the basis of how long you will stay in the grave.

3. Do not look at your delayed appointed time (of death) and your readily available means of living and your belittled sins.

4. Everything will perish except Him. To Him belongs judgment, and to Him will you be brought back (Qur'an 28:88).

 CHAPTER THIRTEEN

1. O child of Adam, many a lamp has been blown out by wind; many a worshipper has been destroyed by pride; many a rich fellow has been destroyed by riches, many a poor fellow has been destroyed by poverty; many a healthy man has been destroyed by good health, and many a learned man has been destroyed by knowledge.

2. But for the very old men who bow down (to Allah in worship), the pious youth, the suckling babies, the grazing animals, the children who read the Qur'an, the Books of Allah, Salatud-duha (the late morning voluntary worship), the women who obey their husbands, waking up to observe night prayer, and those in- charge of Allah's Supreme Authority (lit. those who carry the Throne), I (Allah) would have turned the sky above you into iron, the earth into ice and sand into ashes. I would not have sent down a drop of water from heaven, nor cause any plant to grow in the earth. I would have rained punishments on you torrentially.

 CHAPTER FOURTEEN

1. O child of Adam, your religion is yours, your work is yours, your flesh is yours, and your blood is yours. If your religion becomes bad, your work, flesh, and blood, too, would become bad. But if your religion is good, your work, flesh and blood, too would be good.

2. O child of Adam, do not be like a lamp which burns itself in order to provide light for people. Remove the love of the world from your heart, for surely, I (Allah) will never allow My love and the love of the world to be in the same heart, just as the love of the world and the love of the Hereafter will not stick to the heart of a mu'min (a believer in Allah); same as water and fire will never stay in the same container.

3. O child of Adam, be nice to yourself in your pursuit of wealth, and in amassing vanities. Do not work yourself up, certainly means of livelihood are shared out (by Allah amongst His creature). The covetous shall be denied, the

4. The best wisdom is the fear of Allah. The best wealth is contentment. The best preparation (in life) is consciousness of Allah. The best of what one should harbour in one's mind is certainty of Allah. The best gift is good health. The worst of your weapons is telling lies. The worst advice is slander.

5. And your Lord is not at all unjust to (His) servants. (Qur'an 41:46).

 CHAPTER FIFTEEN

1. O ye who believe, why do you say what you do not do? Most hateful is it in the sight of Allah that you say what you do not do. (Qur'an 61:2).

2. Many a time you forbid certain things to others while you do not forbid them to yourselves.

3. Many a time you enjoin (on others) to do what you do not enjoin on yourselves.

4. Many a time you promise to do one thing and you default. Many a time you amass food which you can not eat. Many a time you nurse an ambition which you can not attain.

5. Many a time you procrastinate repentance day after day, month after month, and year after year. Many a time you look at what Allah made forbidden to you to look at.

6. Have you a guarantee against death or a certificate of exception form Hell-fire has been given to you? Or are you certain that you will succeed in entering Paradise? Is there a pledge between you and (Allah) the Beneficent, the Merciful that He will forgive you your sins?

7. Comfort has made you reckless. Beneficence has spoilt you. Long hope has deceived you against Allah. Do not be

8. Therefore, always be conscious of Allah, O people of understanding, so that you will be successful. Set forth for the remaining part of your life with what you have now.

9. O child of Adam! move forward to Me by the means of your good works, for sure, you have been destroying your life since you came out of your mother while everyday you get closer to your grave until you enter it.

10. O child of Adam, your similitude in this world is like the fly in the honey. When it eats part of it, it clings to it until it dies. You are like that with the world. Therefore, do not be like firewood which burns itself in fire and provides light for others.

 CHAPTER SIXTEEN

1. O child of Adam, do what I (Allah) enjoin you to do and keep away from that which I forbid you. Do not think of yourself as ever-living, who will never die. I am the Living Who will never die: Whenever I desire a thing, I say to it: "Be" and it is.

2. O child of Adam, if your word is sweet while your action is disgusting, know, for sure, that you are the head of the hypocrites. If your physical appearance is beautiful while your interior is ugly, know, for sure, that you are ruined. The word of Allah, the Most Exalted, attests to that by saying: "(They think) that they are deceiving Allah and

3. O child of Adam! none will enter My Paradise except he who humbles himself before My Mightiness, spends his days remembering Me (in whatever he does); restrains himself from lust because of Me; does not harm a stranger; shelters the poor; has mercy for the needy; honours the orphan, and behaves to him like a merciful father, and to widows he behaves like a compassionate husband and a caring merciful brother.

4. Whoever conforms to these descriptions in the world is among My saints and My best friends. If he calls Me I will answer him if he makes a request from Me, I will grant it. Even if he does not make any request from Me, I do not forget him.

 CHAPTER SEVENTEEN

1. O child of Adam! when will you stop complaining about Me (Allah) to My creature? I am not the type of Being one should complain about.

2. How long shall you forget about Me while you always remember others? And I do not deserve that from you. For how long will you be ungrateful to Me whereas I am not unjust to (My) servants. How long will you deny My favour on you whereas there is no other person who can give you (what you desire) other than I.

3. For how long will you trivialize My Book? I do not burden you with what is beyond your capacity. How long do you want to be rude to Me? I am not harsh to you. For how long do you want to give thanks to beings other than Me? Whereas you do not have any Lord other than Me who deserves to be thanked.

4. Whenever you meet a medical doctor you say. (This is) the one who cures my ailment. No one cures you except I (Allah).

5. You say: "our time has done this, our period has done that" I am Time, I am Period. When I send down torrents of rain on you, you say: we have this rain due to so-and-so star. By so doing, you have shown ingratitude to Me while you have believed in stars.

6. (In reality) I am the one who sends down My Mercy upon you in measure, degree, portion, weight, while it is divided (amongst) you.

7. If anyone of you does not get his food for three days, he will say: I am afflicted, I am not fortunate. You have already denied My Favour (on you).

8. Whoever refuses to pay religious tax (zakah) out of his wealth has indeed trivialized My Book. If he knows the time of Salat and is not anxious about it, definitely such a person has neglected Me and is not devoted to Me.

 CHAPTER EIGHTEEN

1. O child of Adam! be patient while you obey Me. Humble yourself before Me and I will elevate you. Be grateful to Me and I will increase My favour on you. Seek My forgiveness and I will forgive you. Call on me, I will answer you. Repent unto Me, I will accept your repentance. Make a request from Me, I will grant it. Give alms for My sake, I will bless your wealth.

2. Maintain good relation with your family, I will prolong your life.

3. Seek good health from Me by observing long silence. Certainly I have. put peace in solitude, while there is sincerity (to Allah) in being pious. Asceticism is in repentance. One serves (Allah best) by not eating one's fill. Why do you crave for wealth without having contentment?

4 O child of Adam! how do you think that you can worship Allah by being gluttonous? How do you desire the love of Allah along with that of the world? How do you want to have illumination of mind while you sleep a lot? How do you fear Allah while you also fear poverty? How do you want to become pious while you are coveteous of the world?

5. How do you desire to attain the pleasure of Allah while you debase the poor? How do you desire to meet Allah while you are miserly? How do you desire to attain wisdom while you love the world, (human) commendation and glorification? How do you desire to attain success with little knowledge?

 CHAPTER NINETEEN

1. O child of Adam! O mankind! There is no way of living which is as good as meditation (on the glory of Allah). There is no piety which is as good as restraining oneself from unlawful things.

2. No noble descent is as good as good behaviour. No lineage is superior to good breeding. No intercessor is as good as repentance.

3. No worship is as good as (the one based on) knowledge. No righteousness is as good as the fear of Allah.

4. No intelligence is as good as patience. No worship is as good as the one which leads to success. No companion is greater than intelligence. No mate is worse than stupidity. No close friend is better than knowledge:

5. O child of Adam! Devote yourself to My worship, I will fill your heart with self-sufficiency; your hands with wealth and your body with comfort.

6. Do not be unmindful of My remembrance lest I fill your heart with poverty, your hands with troubles, your body.

7. If you were to know the remaining part of your life, you would have abandoned the world and would not seek its pleasure.

8. O child of Adam! I strengthened you in order that you will obey Me and do what I make obligatory on you. I also give you wealth and you strengthened yourself with it to disobey Me. I bless you with power and you are able to stand up, sit down, walk about and return home.

9. Under My protection you sleep and wake up. By My grace you live. By My blessing you move up and down. With the good health I give you, you become pleasant-looking.

10. With all these which I have done for you, you forget Me and you remember some one else other than Me. You did not show gratitude to Me.

 CHAPTER TWENTY

1. O child of Adam! Death shall uncover your secrets while the Day of Resurrection shall relate pieces of information about you. The Hereafter and Resurrection shall expose your weak points.

2. Whenever you commit a minor sin, do not look at its being a minor sin, but consider the One. Whom you have sinned against because you do not know by which sin you will provoke His wrath.

3. Whenever you are blessed with a little amount of wealth, do not look at its meagreness but consider Him Who blessed you with it.

4. Do not despise minor sins, because you do not know through which sin you will earn His wrath. (As a consequence), I (Allah) will shut the doors of the heavens against your prayers while you do not know whether I do

 that as a result of great sins or minor ones. Do not feel secure against My strategy. Certainly My strategy is more hidden from you than the crawling (little) ant on a rock in a dark night.

5. O children of Adam! do you remember My wrath when you disobey Me and when you do what I made forbidden to you?

6. Do you do things which I made obligatory on you as I instructed? Do you give the poor their shares out of your wealth? Do you go to those who 'severe' relations with you? Do you deal with honesty those who cheat you? Do you speak to those who dissociate themselves from you?

7. Do you educate your children? Do you please your neighbours? Do you treat fairly those who misbehave to you? Do you forgive those who wrong you?

8. Do you enquire from the learned men about your religion and your worldly life?

9. Certainly, I (Allah) do not look at your make or your beauty but I look at your hearts and I will be pleased with you if these qualities are there. I certainly see all that you do.

 CHAPTER TWENTY-ONE

1. O child of Adam! Reflect on your self and on all My other creatures. If you find any one else superior to you, then transfer your honour to it; but if not, respect yourself by repenting (unto Me). Strengthen yourself with knowledge and good deeds. A support to that is in the words of Allah the Most Exalted which say: "And remembers Allah's favour upon you and the covenant which He made with you, when you say, "we hear and we obey" (Qur'an 5:7)

2. Fear Allah before the Day of Event; the Day of Mutual Loss and Gain; the Day of Reality, the Day which measures fifty thousand years; the Day wherein (man) shall not speak nor will he be allowed to give excuses; the Day of Disaster, the Day of Great Shout, the Day of Frowning and Distress; "and the Day when a soul shall have no power to do anything for another soul. And absolute command on that day will be Allah's". (Qur'an 82:19).

3. The Day of Regret, the Day of Earthquake, the Day of Calamity; and fear Allah before the Day when mountains will move, when punishment will descend and when destruction will be hastened.

4. Fear Allah before the Day of Shout, the Day when there will be no shade except His shade, the Day when infants will grow gray hairs due to violent tremor.

5. And be not like those who say "we hear" but they hear not (Qur'an 8:21).

 CHAPTER TWENTY-TWO

1. O you who believe! remember Allah constantly and glorify Him morning and evening (Qur'an 33:41-42).

2. Allah, the Most Blessed and Exalted, says: O Moses son of 'Imran, O eloquent one, listen to what I tell you. I am the King, the Judge. There is no interpreter between Me and you.

3. Inform whoever practises usury (riba) interest or consumes the wealth of orphans wrongfully, or whoever disobeys Me, or him who is corrupt that he has incurred the wrath of the Beneficent (Allah). He shall be cut off in Hell-fire.

4. O child of Adam! whenever you discover hardness in your heart or disease in your body, or you are deprived of your means of sustenance or decrease in your wealth, you should know then, that you have spoken on an issue that does not concern you.

5. O child of Adam! your religion will never be in order until your tongue is in order. Your tongue will never be in order until your heart is in order. Your heart will not be in order until you feel shy of (disobeying) your Lord.

6. O child of Adam! whenever you look at the faults of people while you forget yours, know that you have pleased satan and disobeyd the Merciful (Allah).

7. O child of Adam! your tongue is your lion. If you let it loose, it will devour you. Your destruction is on the tip of your tongue.

 CHAPTER TWENTY-THREE

1. O children of Adam! certainly, satan is an enemy to you, therefore, treat him as an enemy (by keeping away from him). Know that the day when you will be assembled in groups before Allah, you will stand before Him in rows, while you will read the book (containing your earthly records) in detail. You will be questioned concerning what you did secretly and openly.

2. After that those who were always conscious of Allah while on earth will be piloted to Paradise in groups. The criminals, too, will be dragged to Hell-fire in flocks. Your work will earn you the fulfilment of the reward or threat of punishment from Allah.

3. O child of Adam! surely I am Allah, there is no deity other than I. I have no resemblance. There is no authority other than My Authority.

4. Whoever fasts because of Me for a day in his life I will feed him with various types of food from Paradise (as a reward).

5. Whoever spends a night worshipping Me, I will show a great concern for him. Whoever keeps his eyes away from what I forbid, I will protect him from My Fire.

6. I am the Lord, therefore, know Me. I am the Benefactor; therefore, thank Me. I am the Protector; therefore, seek My protection. I am the Helper, therefore, seek My help. I am the Rich; therefore, seek My gift. I am the Forgiving (Lord); therefore, seek My forgiveness. I am the Goal, therefore aim at Me. I am the Giver; therefore, ask from Me. I am the truly Worshipped, therefore, worship Me. I know what you do secretly and publicly, therefore, beware of Me.

 CHAPTER TWENTY-FOUR

1. Allah bears witness that there is no God save He and (so do) the angels and men of learning. Maintainer of justice, there is no God save He, the Almighty, the Wise. (Qur'an 3:18).

2. He (Allah) places everything in its fitting position. Surely, the religion acceptable to Allah is Islam (that is, complete submission to His will and Guidance - Qur'an 3:19). And who so seeks a religion other than Islam, it will not be accepted from him, and he will be among the losers in the Hereafter. (Qur'an 3:85).

3. O Dawud (David)! give good tidings to everybody who engages in doing absolute goodness that he will enter Paradise. Every evil-doer shall perish. He will perish for disobeying Me.

4. Whoever despairs of My Mercy, I shall burn him in My fire. Whoever knows Allah and obeys Him is safe. And whoever knows satan and disobeyes him is in peace.

5. Whoever knows the truth and follows it is safe. Whoever knows falsehood and shields himself against it is successful. Whoever knows the world and rejects it, has a pure faith. Whoever knows that there is a later life (Hereafter) and seeks it, shall get there (peacefully) surely Allah guides whomsoever He likes, and unto Him shall you all be returned

6. O child of Adam! surely Allah has guaranteed means of living (of His creatures), why then, are you concerned about having excess? When it is true that there is provision from Allah, and that He has guaranteed it, and divided it (among His creatures), why then are you covetous? When it is true that satan is Allah's enemy, why then are you heedless? When it is true that Allah does make substitute, why then are you miserly? When it is true that Allah's punishment is in Hell-fire, why then do you disobey Allah? When it is true that people will walk on Sirat, (it is a narrow slippery bridge across Hell-fire said to be finer than a hair and sharper than a sword while it is beset on each side with hooked thorns every righteous person shall pass through it safely while evil-doers will fall into Hell-fire there), why then do you amass wealth (unjustly)? When it is true that Allah's reward is in Al-Jannah (Paradise), why are you lazy (in worshipping Allah)? When it is true that everything exists according to My Judgment, Decree, and Will, why do you panic?

7. "That you may not despair over what is lost to you nor exult because of that which He bestows on you. And Allah loves not any self-conceited boaster" (Qur'an 57:23).

 CHAPTER TWENTY-FIVE

1. O child of Adam! make much provision, certainly the road is rough while the journey is long. Make provision for the day when you will stand before Allah. Surely, the Creator is wise.

2. Let the boat be in good condition; surely the sea is deep. Let the load be light; surely the road is delicate. Purify your work; surely the (Omniscient) Critic is watching.

3. Delay your (long) sleep till the grave, your joy till (when your deeds are put on) the scale; your passion till (you enter) Paradise, your attainment of Grace till the Hereafter and your enjoyment till you meet the comely companions of Paradise.

4. Be Mine and I will be yours. Move close to Me by despising the world, by moving far away from it, by hating the evil-doers and by loving the upright ones.

5. "Surely, Allah suffers not the reward of those who do good to be lost". (Qur'an 9:120).

 CHAPTER TWENTY-SIX

1. O children of Adam! how long shall you continue to disobey Me and you are unhappy about the heat of the sun, scorching ground and the heat of Jahannam (Hell-fire). Jahannam has seven layers of fire; a part of it rages into the other. In each of the layers there are seventy thousand valleys of fire.

2. In each of the valleys, there are seventy thousand branches of fire. In each branch, there are seventy thousand abodes of fire. In each abode, there are seventy thousand houses of fire. In each house, there are seventy thousand wells of fire.

3. In each well, there are seventy thousand boxes of fire. In each box, there are seventy thousand Zaqqum trees producing bitter and poisonous fruits. Under each tree, there are seventy thousand shackles of fire. In each of the shackles there are seventy thousand chains of fire.

4. In each chain there are seventy thousand snakes of fire.

 The length of each snake is seventy thousand arm's lengths. In the stomach of every snake, there is a sea of black venom. The Hell-fire also contains seventy thousand scorpions of fire. Each scorpion has seventy thousand tails of fire. Each tail contains seventy thousand spines of fire. Each spine contains seventy thousand rotl (210,000kgs) of red venom.

5. I swear by My Name, the Mount (Sinai), the Book written on parchment unfolded, the often frequented House (Ka'abah), the roof elevated, the sea filled (to capacity). The punishment of your Lord shall surely come to pass. There shall be none to avert it. (Qur'an 52:1-8)

6. O child of Adam! I did not create these sets of fire except for every kafir (unbeliever in Allah), miser, slanderer, one who disobeys his parents (recalcitrant child), great liar, envier, faultfinder, calumniator, pimp, highway robber, corrupt law officer, and effeminate.

7. It is created also for every one who destroys family ties, one who swears in Allah's name falsely, traitor, quarrelsome, usurer, one who abandons salat (ritual prayers) even though he is healthy, adulterer, one who refuses to pay Zakat (religious tax) out of his wealth.

8. (Others include every) one who hurts his neighbours, perfidious person, drinker of alcohol, adulterous old man, embezzler of orphan's property, professional female (or male) honourer, one who forgets the Qur'an after memorizing it, and profligate person.

9. (The aforementioned people will enter fire) except those who repent, have faith and engage in good deeds. Those are they who Allah will change their bad deeds to good ones; and Allah is Most Forgiving, Merciful. Qur'an 25:70.

10. O my servants! have mercy on yourselves, your body is weak, while the journey is long. The load is heavy and the road is delicate. Israfil is the Trumpeter. The Hell-fire is blazing. The Judge is (Allah) the Lord of the worlds.

 CHAPTER TWENTY SEVEN

1. O mankind! beware of having desire for the world because it is a transient comfort, an ephemeral life, and an evanescent passion.

2. I surely have with Me, for My obedient servants, Paradise which have eight gates.

3. In each Paradise there are seventy thousand meadows of saffron. Each meadow also contains seventy thousand cities made of pearls and corals. Each city contains seventy thousand palaces made of sapphire. Each palace consists of seventy thousand buildings made of red chrysolite.

4. Each building contains seventy thousand houses made of red gold and each house contains seventy thousand dining-shops made of white silver while each dining-shop contains seventy thousand dining-tables made of honey. There are seventy thousand dishes made of jewel on each dining-table. In each dish there are seventy thousand different types of food.

5. Around each dining-room, there are seventy thousand beds made of corals. On each bed there are seventy thousand mattresses made of silk, silk brocade, sarcenet, and brocade.

6. Around each bed there are seventy thousand rivers containing water of life, milk, alcohol and pure honey. 7. In the middle of each river, there are seventy thousand different types of fruits. In each house, there are seventy thousand tents made of jewels. There are seventy thousand beds in each tent. On each bed there are seventy thousand hours with pleasant, lovely eyes. 8. Standing before it are seventy thousand ladies in waiting who look like well-preserved hidden eggs. 9. On the top of each palace, there are seventy thousand domes made of camphor. In each dome, there are seventy thousand gifts from (Allah) the Most Beneficent. Therein are things which no eye has ever seen, no ear has ever heard and no mind has ever thought of. 10. (Other things reserved for the inmates of Paradise) are fruits that they prefer, flesh of birds that they desire, and hours (beautiful ladies with wide, lovely eyes) who look like hidden pearls. (They will be given these) as a reward for what they did. (Qur'an 56:20-24). 11. Whoever enters My Paradise (Al-Jannah) will never die therein, he will never age; he will never worry; he will never be sad; he will not transact any business with anybody. He will not cry. He will not fast. He will not pray salat (ritual prayer). He will not be sick. He will not be distressed. He will not go to toilet. He will not urinate. they (the inmates) will not quit it. 12. Whoever seeks My Pleasure, let him seek it by pleasing the poor; then, I shall be pleased with you.

13. Whoever seeks the Home of My Honour (Paradise) My neighbourhood (nearness to Me), and My Blessing, let him move near Me by giving out alms, by despising the world and by being satisfied with little (sustenance).

 CHAPTER TWENTY-EIGHT

1. O child of Adam! Wealth is Mine. Paradise is Mine. And all of you are My servants. O My servants! purchase My Paradise with My Wealth. I surely have many Gardens in My Paradise for those who obey Me.

2. Your real share of My Wealth is what you (personally) consume and exhaust, or what you use in buying clothes which you wear till they are worn out, or what you give out as alms and it is well spent. Anything other than these you will account for it while its reward will go to another person. Your real share of your wealth is what you take out of it and spend.

3. Your essence is in three parts. One is for Me, one is for you while one is in-between Me and you. The one which is for Me is your spirit. The one which is for you is your work, while the one which stands between Me and you is your supplication - you pray to Me and the acceptance of the prayer rests on Me.

4. O child of Adam! be cautious, then you will know Me. Come back to Me, then you will see Me. Worship Me, then you will reach Me. Seek Me, and you will find Me.

5. O child of Adam! when the people in authority will enter Hell-fire because of injustice, the Arabs (or any other race) because of disobedience (to Allah), the scholars because of envy, the traders because of cheating, the soldiers because of ignorance, the manufacturer/craftsman because of fraud, the worshippers because of ostentation, the rich because of pride, the poor because of lie, who then is seeking paradise? 

6. They will not enter Paradise except through My Mercy.

 CHAPTER TWENTY-NINE

1. O you who believe! fear Allah as He should be feared; and let not death overtake you except when you are in a state of complete submission (to the will of Allah - Qur'an 3:102).

2. O child of Adam! the similitude of unutilized knowledge is like thunder and lightning unaccompanied by rain. Also, the unutilized knowledge is like a tree without fruits. Again, unutilized knowledge is like a bow without a string. Unutilized knowledge is also like a man who sows wheat on rock.

3. The similitude of knowledge possessed by a fool is like pearls and sapphire owned by animals. And the similitude of a hardened heart with knowledge is like a stone placed in water. And the similitude of admonition given to somebody who does not want it is like playing clarinet at a graveyard.

4. The similitude of a wealth given out as alms but derived from unlawful source is like somebody washing excreta on his clothe with his urine. The similitude of someone's ritual prayer who does not pay religious tax (zakat) is like a body without soul.

5. The knowledge of someone who does not repent is like a house built without foundation.

6. "And none feels secure from Allah's strategy except the people who shall perish". (Qur'an 7:99).

 CHAPTER THIRTY

1. O child of Adam! obey Me in exact degree as your heart is inclined to the world, and if you will not do that, then remove My love from your heart. Surely, I will never allow My love and the love for the world to remain together in one heart, just as water and fire will never stay together in one container.

2. A child of Adam! be cautious, then you will know Me. Worship Me, then you will get to Me. Return to Me, then you will see Me. Worship Me, and devote yourself exclusively to My worship. Be faithful in your work to Me and free it from ostentation. Establish My love in your heart, and devote yourself to My remembrance, I will then mention you to My angels.

3. O child of Adam! many a time you will stand before Allah while you think of another thing. If you had known the reality of Allah, you would not have concerned yourself with something different from Allah and you would fear nothing except Allah.

4. You mention other thing other than Allah with your tongue. Do not backbite anybody. Whenever you commit a sin, seek the forgiveness of Allah. There is no forgiveness for he who persists in sinful acts. The seeking of forgiveness while one persists in committing sins is the repentance of liars. "And your Lord is not unjust to His servants". (Qur'an 41:46).

5. O child of Adam! if you fear Hell-fire as you fear poverty I would have enriched you from sources which are not known to you. If you have desired Al-Jannah (Paradise) as you desire the world, I would have made you happy in both places.

6. If you had remembered Me as you remember one another, My angels would have been sending greetings to you morning and evening.

7. If you had loved to worship Me as you love the world, I would have blessed you as I bless My prophets and apostles.

8. Do not allow your hearts to be inclined towards loving the world, for it will soon come to an end.

9. When each one of you is placed on the edge of his grave he shall be called three times and shall be told: O child of Adam! come forward to meet (the result) of the work you put forth. If it was good (the result) shall be good. But if it was bad, (the result) shall be bad.

10. When he is put in his grave, he shall be called three times and told: "O child of Adam! you had been walking on it (the earth) and today, you are inside it, sad and crying.

11. When dust is put on him, he will be called thrice saying to him: O child of Adam! they buried you and abandoned you. They are of no benefit to you.

12. You amassed wealth and left it behind for others whereas you will account for it. The good reward that comes out of it shall be in another person's account.

13. Today, are you going to the Exalted Paradise? If it is, then I congratulate you.

14. Or you are going into a hot Hell-fire? Then you will be sad.
EXCELLENT EXHORTATIONS.
I wonder for the one who is certain that there is death, and yet laughs.
I wonder for the one who is certain that the grave exists and yet rejoices.
I wonder for the one who is certain that there is accountability, and yet accumulates wealth.
I wonder for the one who is certain that the grave exists with its narrowness, and yet laughs (hysterically)!
I wonder for the one who is certain that there is life after death and its woe, pleasure  and eternity and  yet does forget about it.
I wonder for the one who is certain that there is Hellfire and its chastisement and yet sleeps without fleeing from it.
I wonder for the one who is certain that there is Paradise and its pleasure, and yet sleeps without seeking it.
I wonder for the one who is certain about this world and its transience (lasts only for a short time) and yet trusts in it implicitly (without a doubt or question)!
I wonder for the one who is certain that he is learned verbally but ignorant in mind how he shall succeed (before ALLAH)!
I wonder for the one who is sad over the decrease in his wealth while he is not sad over the decrease in his life!
I wonder for the one who used water to clean himself while he is not clean at Heart!
I wonder for the one who is preoccupied by the faults of others while he forgets about his own faults.
I wonder for the one who knows ALLAH sees him and yet disobeys Him!
I wonder for the one who knows that he will die alone, be buried alone and be made to render account (of his deeds) alone and ye socializes with people . Why doesn't he spend his time with his LORD and His Messenger by saying (always) : There is not Deity but ALLAH, Muhammad is truly and sincerely the Messenger of ALLAH.
TAKEN FROM ZABURA

THE LIQUID DROP CONCENTRATION METHOD.


Hdith Qudsi  
The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said : "When Allah decreed the Creation He  
pledged Himself by writing in His book which is laid down with Him : My mercy  
prevails over my wrath."  
The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said : "Allah Almighty has said : 'The son  
of Adam denied Me and he had no right to do so. And he reviled Me and he had no right  
to do so. As for his denying Me, it is his saying: He will not remake me as He made me at  
first (1) - and the initial creation \[of him\] is no easier for Me than remaking him. As for his  
reviling Me, it is his saying: Allah has taken to Himself a son, while I am the One, the  
Everlasting Refuge. I begot not nor was I begotten, and there is none comparable to Me."  
(1) i.e., bring me back to life after death.  
The Messenger of Allah (may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon him) led the  
morning prayer for us at al-Hudaybiyah following rainfall during the night. When the  
Prophet (may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon him) finished, he faced the people  
and said to them : "Do you know what your Lord has said?" They said : "Allah and his  
Messenger know best". He said : "This morning one of my servants became a believer in  
me and one a disbeliever. As for him who said : We have been given rain by virtue of  
Allah and His mercy, that one is a believer in me, a disbeliever in the stars (1); and as for  
him who said : We have been given rain by such-and-such a star, that one is a disbeliever  
in Me, a believer in the stars."  
(1) The pre-Islamic Arabs believed that rain was brought about by the movement of stars.  
This Hadith draws attention to the fact that whatever be the direct cause of such natural  
phenomena as rain, it is Allah the Almighty who is the Disposer of all things  
The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said : "Allah said : 'Sons of Adam inveigh  
against \[the vicissitudes of\] Time, and I am Time, in My hand is the night and the day  
(1).'"  
(1) As the Almighty is the Ordainer of all things, to inveigh aginst misfortunes that are part  
of Time is tantamount to inveighing against Him.  
Reporters.  
The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said : "Allah (glorified and exalted be He)  
said : I am so self-sufficient that I am in no need of having an associate. Thus he who  
does an action for someone else's sake as well as Mine will have that action renounced by  
Me to him whom he associated with Me."  
I heard the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) say : "The first of people against  
whom judgment will be pronounced on the Day of Resurrection will be a man who died a  
martyr. He will be brought and Allah will make known to him His favours and he will  
recognize them. \[The Almighty\] will say : And what did you do about them? He will say :  
I fought for you until I died a martyr. He will say: You have lied - you did but fight that it  
might be said \[of you\]: He is courageous. And so it was said. Then he will be ordered to  
be dragged along on his face until he is cast into Hell-fire. \[Another\] will be a man who  
has studied \[religious\] knowledge and has taught it and who used to recite the Quran. He  
will be brought and Allah will make known to his His favours and he will recognize  
them. \[The Almighty\] will say: And what did you do about them? He will say: I studied  
\[religious\] knowledge and I taught it and I recited the Quran for Your sake. He will say:  
You have lied - you did but study \[religious\] knowledge that it might be said \[of you\]: He  
is learned. And you recited the Quran that it might be said \[of you\]: He is a reciter. And  
so it was said. Then he will be ordered to be dragged along on his face until he is cast into  
Hell-fire. \[Another\] will be a man whom Allah had made rich and to whom He had given  
all kinds of wealth. He will be brought and Allah will make known to his His favours and  
he will recognize them. \[The Almighty\] will say: And what did you do about them? He   
will say: I left no path \[untrodden\] in which You like money to be spent without spending  
in it for Your sake. He will say: You have lied - you did but do so that it might be said \[of  
you\]: He is open-handed. And so it was said. Then he will be ordered to be dragged along  
on his face until he is cast into Hell-fire."  
I heard the messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) say : "Your Lord delights at a  
shepherd who, on the peak of a mountain crag, gives the call to prayer and prays. Then  
Allah (glorified and exalted be He) say: Look at this servant of Mine, he gives the call to  
prayer and performs the prayers; he is in awe of Me. I have forgiven My servant \[his sins\]  
and have admitted him to Paradise."  
From the Prophet (peace be upon him), who said :  
"A prayer performed by someone who has not recited the Essence of the Quran (1) during  
it is deficient (and he repeated the word three times), incomplete. Someone said to Abu  
Hurayrah: \[Even though\] we are behind the imam? (2) He said: Recite it to yourself, for I  
have heard the Prophet (may the blessings and peace of Allah be up on him) say: Allah  
(mighty and sublime be He), had said: I have divided prayer between Myself and My  
servant into two halves, and My servant shall have what he has asked for. When the  
servant says: Al-hamdu lillahi rabbi l-alamin (3), Allah (mighty and sublime be He) says:  
My servant has praised Me. And when he says: Ar-rahmani r-rahim (4), Allah (mighty and  
sublime be He) says: My servant has extolled Me, and when he says: Maliki yawmi d-din  
(5), Allah says: My servant has glorified Me - and on one occasion He said: My servant  
has submitted to My power. And when he says: Iyyaka na budu wa iyyaka nasta in (6), He  
says: This is between Me and My servant, and My servant shall have what he has asked  
for. And when he says: Ihdina s-sirata l- mustaqim, siratal ladhina an amta alayhim  
ghayril-maghdubi alayhim wa la d-dallin (7), He says: This is for My servant, and My  
servant shall have what he has asked for."  
(1) Surat al-Fatihah, the first surah (chapter) of the Qur'an.  
(2) i.e. standing behind the imam (leader) listening to him reciting al-Fatihah.  
(3) "Praise be to Allah, Lord of the Worlds."  
(4) "The Merciful, the Compassionate".  
(5) "Master of the Day of Judgement".  
"It is You we worship and it is You we ask for help".  
(7) "Guide us to the straight path, the path of those upon whom You have bestowed favors,  
not of those against whom You are angry, nor of those who are astray".  
From the Prophet (peace be upon him), who said : "Allah (mighty and sublime be He)  
says : The first of his actions for which a servant of Allah will be held accountable on the  
Day of Resurrection will be his prayers. If they are in order, then he will have prospered  
and succeeded: and if they are wanting, then he will have failed and lost. If there is  
something defective in his obligatory prayers, the Lord (glorified and exalted be He) will  
say: See if My servant has any supererogatory prayers with which may be completed that  
which was defective in his obligatory prayers. Then the rest of his actions will be judged  
in like fashion."  
From the Prophet (peace be upon him), who said : "Allah (mighty and sublime be He)  
says : Fasting is Mine and it I who give reward for it. \[A man\] gives up his sexual  
passion, his food and his drink for my sake. Fasting is like a shield, and he who fasts has  
two joys: a joy whin he breaks his fast and a joy when he meets his Lord. The change in  
the breath of the mouth of him who fasts is better in Allah's estimation than the smell of  
musk." From the Prophet (peace be upon him), who said : "Allah (mighty and sublime be He)  
said : Spend (on charity), O son of Adam, and I shall spend on you."  
The Messenger of Allah (may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon him) said : "A  
man from among those who were before you was called to account. Nothing in the way of  
good was found for him except that he used to have dealings with people and, being well  
to-do, he would order his servants to let off the man in straitened circumstances \[from  
repaying his debt\]. He (the Prophet p.b.u.h) said that Allah said : We are worthier than  
you of that (of being so generous). Let him off."   
I was with the Messenger of Allah (may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon him)  
and there came to him two men : one of them was complaining of penury (being very  
poor), while the other was complaining of brigandry (robbery). The Messenger of Allah  
(may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon him) said : "As for brigandry, it will be but  
a short time before a caravan will \[be able to\] go out of Mecca without a guard. As for  
penury, the Hour (Day of Judgement) will not arrive before one of you takes his charity  
around without finding anyone to accept it from him. Then (1) one of you will surely stand  
before Allah, there being no screed between Him and him, nor an interpreter to translate  
for him. Then He will say to him: Did I not bring you wealth? And he will say: Yes. Then  
He will say: Did I not send to you a messenger? And he will say: Yes. And he will look to  
his right and will see nothing but Hell-fire, then he will look to his left and will see  
nothing but Hell-fire, so let each of you protect himself against Hell-fire, be it with even  
half a date - and if he finds it not, then with a kind word."  
(1) i.e. at the time of the Hour.  
From the Prophet (peace be upon him), who said : "Allah (glorified and exalted be He)  
has supernumerary angels who rove about seeking out gatherings in which Allah's name  
is being invoked: they sit with them and fold their wings round each other, fillin that  
which is between them and between the lowest heaven. When \[the people in the  
gathering\] depart, \[the angels\] ascend and rise up to heaven. He (the Prophet p.b.u.h.) said: Then Allah (mighty and sublime be He) asks them - \[though\] He is most knowing  
about them: From where have you come? And they say: We have come from some  
servants of Yours on Earth: they were glorifying You (Subhana llah), exalting you  
(Allahu akbar), witnessing that there is no god but You (La ilaha illa llah), praising You  
(Al-Hamdu lillah), and asking \[favours\] of You. He says: And what do they ask of Me?  
They say: They ask of You Your Paradise. He says: And have they seen My Paradise?  
They say: No, O Lord. He says: And how would it be were they to have seen My  
Paradise! They say: And they ask protection of You. He says: From what do they ask  
protection of Me? They say: From Your Hell-fire, O Lord. He says: And have they seen  
My Hell-fire? They say: NO. He says: And how would it be were they to have seen My  
Hell-fire: They say: And they ask for Your forgiveness. He (the Prophet p.b.u.h) said:  
Then He says: I have forgiven them and I have bestowed upon them what they have asked  
for,and I have granted them sanctuary from that from which they asked protection. He  
(the Prophet p.b.u.h) said: They say: O Lord, among then is So-and-so, a much sinning  
servant, who was merely passing by and sat down with them. He (the Prophet p.b.u.h)  
said: And He says: And to him \[too\] I have given forgiveness: he who sits with such  
people shall not suffer."  
The Prophet (peace be upon him) said : "Allah the Almighty said : I am as My servant  
thinks I am (1). I am with him when he makes mention of Me. If he makes mention of Me  
to himself, I make mention of him to Myself; and if he makes mention of Me in an  
assembly, I make mention of him in an assemble better than it. And if he draws near to  
Me an arm's length, I draw near to him a fathom's length. And if he comes to Me walking,  
I go to him at speed."  
(1) Another possible rendering of the Arabic is: "I am as My servant expects Me to be".  
The meaning is that forgiveness and acceptance of repentance by the Almighty is subject  
to His servant truly believing that He is forgiving and merciful. However, not to  
accompany such belief with right action would be to mock the Almighty.  
From the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him), among the sayings he related from his  
Lord (glorified and exalted be He) is that He said : "Allah has written down the good   
deeds and the bad ones. Then He explained it \[by saying that\] he who has intended a good  
deed and has not done it, Allah writes it down with Himself as a full good deed, but if he  
has intended it and has done it, Allah writes it down with Himself as from ten good deeds  
to seven hundred times, or many times over. But if he has intended a bad deed and has
